### Leclerc triumphs at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 24*

_Leclerc delivered a controlled drive to win the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix ahead of Max Verstappen and George Russell._

There was a composed inevitability to Leclerc's win at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix. Leclerc had 15.9s in hand over Verstappen at the chequered flag, George Russell in third. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Leclerc took every one of them.

Verstappen led the early laps only for Leclerc to find a way ahead as the strategy unfolded. Verstappen had grabbed pole by 0.187s from Leclerc. The standout recovery came from Sergio Larson, who advanced 6 places to finish 5th. Leclerc executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

Only Oscar Piastri failed to make the flag, terminal floor damage the cause.

No change at the top, Max Verstappen on 495 points with Charles Leclerc 50 points adrift.

"I knew the gap was there if I could hold the pace, and the team gave me the right call at the right time," said Leclerc.

### Verstappen moves clear after the Qatar Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 23*

_A composed afternoon from Verstappen puts Red Bull on the top step at the Qatar Grand Prix._

Max Verstappen added the Qatar Grand Prix to his record, seeing off the pressure from the pack behind. Charles Leclerc crossed the line 34.5s adrift, Lando Norris completing the top three. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Verstappen took every one of them.

Verstappen made the most of pole, getting away cleanly and building a gap before the first stops. Verstappen had grabbed pole by 0.047s from Leclerc. The standout recovery came from Oliver Bearman, who advanced 5 places to finish 8th. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Verstappen found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 4 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Dion Gunther, Liam Lawson, Lance Stroll and George Russell all failed to see the flag.

With the win, Max Verstappen can no longer be caught in the championship.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Verstappen.

### Oscar Piastri pulling clear of Leonard Grube

*Analysis · Round 23*

_The intra-team balance has tilted at Alpine._

Oscar Piastri and Leonard Grube share a pit wall and an engineering group, yet the scoreboard shows Oscar Piastri on 51 against Grube's 0. A gap of that magnitude between teammates is not noise; it reflects a consistent edge in race execution and qualifying trim.

And it is only widening, with Piastri outscoring Grube 16 to 0 over the last 6 races.

For Grube, the most damaging consequence is not the points gap itself but the internal leverage it hands to Piastri when engineering resources are allocated. Every race weekend Grube fails to close the gap, the burden of expectation compounds.

The internal pecking order at Alpine is hardening into something that will be difficult for Grube to overturn without a clear step forward in raw qualifying pace. Grube must identify whether the deficit is mechanical setup, tyre management, or racecraft, because the fix differs in each case.

"I am not panicking, we keep working," said Grube.

### The seats drawing attention with the season nearly over

*Silly season · Round 23*

_5 moves are commanding attention in the paddock with the season nearly over._

Driver talk has sharpened with the season nearly over, with 5 moves circulating and Dion Gunther attracting the loudest noise.

Speculation over a Dion Gunther departure from Racing Bulls to Ferrari has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Racing Bulls currently sit 9th while Ferrari occupy 5th. What Ferrari are buying, if this goes through, is Gunther's raw potential, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Gunther room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Speculation over a Pierre Gasly departure from Aston Martin to Williams has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Aston Martin currently sit 11th while Williams occupy 8th. What Williams are buying, if this goes through, is Gasly's reliability between the walls, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Gasly room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

A move linking Sergio Larson with Mercedes has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Mercedes are 6th, behind Ferrari in 5th. Larson's 57 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Mercedes, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Larson's deal expiring, Ferrari face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Larson acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

A move linking Arvid Lindblad with Alpine has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 8th at Williams to 7th at Alpine. Inside Alpine, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Lindblad's deal expiring, Williams face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Lindblad acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

Speculation over a Carlos Sainz departure from Williams to Audi has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Williams in 8th, Audi in 10th. What Audi are buying, if this goes through, is Sainz's big-race temperament, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Sainz room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Everything here is smoke for now, though silly season has a reliable habit of producing fire.

### Charles Leclerc masters the Las Vegas Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 22*

_Charles Leclerc claimed his 8th win of the season at the Las Vegas Grand Prix._

Charles Leclerc delivered at the Las Vegas Grand Prix, holding the lead through the phases that decide races. Leclerc had 3.0s in hand over Verstappen at the chequered flag, Lando Norris in third. It was 25 points banked for Leclerc and Red Bull.

From the front row Leclerc dictated every phase, using the clean air to extend the lead at will. Leclerc had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.064s. Red Bull committed to a one-stop strategy from the outset and Leclerc drove it to perfection.

2 cars did not make the finish, thinning the points-paying places as the race wore on. Out of the running were Ocon with a power-unit failure and Antonelli with a high-speed spin into the barriers.

Max Verstappen holds the points lead on 452, 50 points up on Charles Leclerc.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Leclerc.

### Verstappen sees off Norris to win the Sao Paulo Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 21*

_Max Verstappen took victory at the Sao Paulo Grand Prix, with Lando Norris and George Russell completing the podium._

Max Verstappen took victory at the Sao Paulo Grand Prix in a drive that answered every question put to him. The gap to Lando Norris at the flag was 75.3s, George Russell rounding out the podium behind. A maximum 25-point haul was the reward for the drive.

Verstappen converted pole the efficient way, never relinquishing the lead and managing the gap as required. Verstappen had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.039s. The win was built on a two-stop strategy, with Verstappen making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

6 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Piastri with damage from a first-lap clash, Leclerc with a power-unit failure, Grube with an engine that let go, Moors with an electrical failure, Hamilton with a hydraulics leak and Gunther with a high-speed spin into the barriers.

Piastri sat quietly for a while before facing anyone.

Atop the standings, Max Verstappen keeps a 57-point cushion over Charles Leclerc.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Verstappen.

### Max Verstappen has the upper hand at Red Bull

*Analysis · Round 21*

_Max Verstappen leads Charles Leclerc 434 to 377 at Red Bull._

A 57-point margin separates Max Verstappen from Charles Leclerc at Red Bull, the widest the intra-team gulf has been this season. A gap of that magnitude between teammates is not noise; it reflects a consistent edge in race execution and qualifying trim.

The recent trend is grim for Leclerc, beaten 36 to 75 on points over the last 3 races.

For Leclerc, the most damaging consequence is not the points gap itself but the internal leverage it hands to Verstappen when engineering resources are allocated. The question for Leclerc is whether the gap gets addressed through performance or rationalised through excuses, and the paddock is watching for which answer emerges.

From Verstappen's perspective the pattern is straightforward: translate car pace into points more efficiently than Leclerc, round after round. Until Leclerc can outscore Verstappen on consecutive weekends, the gap will remain the story inside the Red Bull garage.

"I am not panicking, we keep working," said Leclerc.

### Verstappen delivers at the Mexico City Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 20*

_Max Verstappen claimed his 13th win of the season at the Mexico City Grand Prix._

There was a composed inevitability to Verstappen's win at the Mexico City Grand Prix. Verstappen finished 9.6s clear of Charles Leclerc, with George Russell a further step back in third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Red Bull.

Verstappen led from the front after taking pole, controlling the tempo and never inviting a challenge. Verstappen had grabbed pole by 0.061s from Leclerc. Nobody gained more ground than Kimi Antonelli, 5 places from 13th to 8th in a drive that caught the eye. Verstappen executed a three-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

Only Lewis Hamilton failed to make the flag, a high-speed spin into the barriers the cause.

No change at the top, Max Verstappen on 409 points with Charles Leclerc 32 points adrift.

"These points matter. Every race this season has felt like it counts, and today was no different," said Verstappen.

### The driver market stirs as the campaign enters its closing stretch

*Silly season · Round 18*

_5 moves are commanding attention in the paddock as the campaign enters its closing stretch._

The market has teeth as the campaign enters its closing stretch, and Arvid Lindblad is the name that keeps surfacing above the rest.

Arvid Lindblad has emerged as a target for Ferrari. The gap between 8th and 5th in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. The attraction for Ferrari is clear: Lindblad would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Lindblad reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Sources inside Cadillac describe Pierre Gasly as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Aston Martin are 11th, Cadillac are 4th. Cadillac see Gasly's all-round package as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Gasly here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Gasly in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Sergio Larson has emerged as a target for Williams. Strange optics attach to this one: Ferrari sit 5th, Williams sit 8th. The attraction for Williams is clear: Larson would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Larson reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Sources inside Mercedes describe Dion Gunther as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Racing Bulls are 9th, Mercedes are 6th. Mercedes see Gunther's marketability as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Gunther here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

Speculation over a Sebastian Lehner departure from Audi to Racing Bulls has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Audi currently sit 10th while Racing Bulls occupy 9th. What Racing Bulls are buying, if this goes through, is Lehner's fresh edge, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Lehner room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

The rumour mill turns faster than the contract pen, and plenty will change before ink dries.

Verstappen led from the front after taking pole, controlling the tempo and never inviting a challenge. Verstappen had grabbed pole by 0.142s from Leclerc. Red Bull committed to a two-stop strategy from the outset and Verstappen drove it to perfection.

4 cars did not make the finish, thinning the points-paying places as the race wore on. Out of the running were Bearman with a power-unit failure, Lindblad with a wheel-nut problem at a stop, Gasly with an oil leak and Lawson with a hydraulics leak.

Bearman's walk back to the garage said everything about a day that promised points and delivered none.

Max Verstappen holds the points lead on 259, 15 points up on Charles Leclerc.

### Paddock whispers grow louder at the midway point of the season

*Silly season · Round 12*

_7 moves are commanding attention in the paddock at the midway point of the season._

Driver talk has sharpened at the midway point of the season, with 7 moves circulating and George Russell attracting the loudest noise.

Speculation over a George Russell departure from McLaren to Red Bull has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: McLaren currently sit 2nd while Red Bull occupy 1st. A tally of 174 points has done the reputation no harm. What Red Bull are buying, if this goes through, is Russell's all-round package, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Russell room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Russell said, holding the line.

Charles Leclerc has emerged as a target for McLaren. Strange optics attach to this one: Red Bull sit 1st, McLaren sit 2nd. Put plainly, 226 points is the number that has put Leclerc on shortlists. The attraction for McLaren is clear: Leclerc would add a proven race winner to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Leclerc reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Speculation over a Carlos Sainz departure from Williams to Racing Bulls has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Williams in 8th, Racing Bulls in 9th. What Racing Bulls are buying, if this goes through, is Sainz's reliability between the walls, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Sainz room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Sainz said, holding the line.

Sources inside Ferrari describe Esteban Ocon as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 4th to 5th, which demands explanation. The ledger reads 26 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Ferrari see Ocon's reliability between the walls as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Ocon here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

A move linking Sergio Larson with Cadillac has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 5th at Ferrari to 4th at Cadillac. Larson's 20 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Cadillac, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Larson's deal expiring, Ferrari face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Larson acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

Speculation over a Pierre Gasly departure from Aston Martin to Audi has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Aston Martin currently sit 11th while Audi occupy 10th. What Audi are buying, if this goes through, is Gasly's racecraft and composure, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Gasly room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Gasly said, holding the line.

Speculation over a Dion Gunther departure from Racing Bulls to Alpine has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Racing Bulls currently sit 9th while Alpine occupy 7th. What Alpine are buying, if this goes through, is Gunther's raw potential, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Gunther room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Everything here is smoke for now, though silly season has a reliable habit of producing fire.

### Leclerc delivers at the British Grand Prix

_Charles Leclerc claimed his 5th win of the season at the British Grand Prix._

Charles Leclerc claimed the British Grand Prix with a controlled performance that the chasing pack could not match. Max Verstappen crossed the line 2.5s adrift, Franco Colapinto completing the top three. There was nothing left on the table, 25 points and the win for Leclerc.

Verstappen set the pace in qualifying, but Leclerc reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Verstappen by 0.060s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. The retirement count reached 2 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Bearman with an electrical failure and Ocon with an oil leak.

Leclerc stood on the podium with the look of someone who knew the result had been earned, not gifted.

"I knew the gap was there if I could hold the pace, and the team gave me the right call at the right time," said Leclerc.

### Red Bull claim the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix through Verstappen

*Race report · Round 5*

_Max Verstappen took victory at the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix, with George Russell and Charles Leclerc completing the podium._

Max Verstappen was the class of the field at the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix, turning qualifying pace into race-day victory. Verstappen finished 13.7s clear of George Russell, with Charles Leclerc a further step back in third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Red Bull.

Verstappen made the most of pole, getting away cleanly and building a gap before the first stops. Verstappen had grabbed pole by 0.027s from Leclerc. Liam Lawson produced the drive of the afternoon, charging from 9th into the points in 5th. The win was built on a one-stop strategy, with Verstappen making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

3 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Hamilton with a power-unit failure, Norris with an engine that let go and Hadjar with a high-speed spin into the barriers.

Verstappen drove the in-lap at a measured pace, in no rush to let the afternoon end.

Atop the standings, Max Verstappen keeps a 22-point cushion over Charles Leclerc.

### Verstappen delivers at the Bahrain Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 4*

_A composed afternoon from Verstappen puts Red Bull on the top step at the Bahrain Grand Prix._

Max Verstappen claimed the Bahrain Grand Prix with a controlled performance that the chasing pack could not match. The gap to George Russell at the flag was 19.6s, Mike Moors rounding out the podium behind. A maximum 25-point haul was the reward for the drive.

Leclerc set the pace in qualifying, but Verstappen reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Leclerc by 0.187s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. Nobody gained more ground than Sergio Larson, 4 places from 9th to 5th in a drive that caught the eye. Verstappen executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The retirement count reached 3 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Colapinto with brake failure, Gunther with a suspension failure and Leclerc with a power-unit failure.

Verstappen stood on the podium with the look of someone who knew the result had been earned, not gifted.

The points lead changes hands, Max Verstappen now in front of Charles Leclerc by 12 points.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Verstappen.

### Winter speed tells a story for 2031

*Preview · Pre-season*

_The 2031 grid has sorted itself early, with Red Bull at the top and McLaren the only side close enough to make it a genuine fight._

What Red Bull have shown in testing is not merely a single-lap flier but a consistent race-trim performance that signals a car built to win across a long calendar.

The midfield is packed tightly enough that a single successful upgrade package could vault a team from sixth in the constructors' standings to third, making the chasing positions as contested as the front.

For McLaren, the task is narrowing the gap to Red Bull fast enough that a title fight is still mathematically alive when the calendar turns to its final third.

---

### 2031 championship hopefuls pull back the wraps

*Launch · Pre-season*

_Red Bull heads a group of 3 teams whose cars were built with one purpose, reaching the top step._

Scrutiny falls on 3 teams as the fastest expected cars of 2031 make their public debut. Red Bull may lead the conversation, but the entire group has arrived with something to say.

Every aero surface on Red Bull's 2031 contender is new, and Charles Leclerc and Max Verstappen have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. a 2nd-place finish last season set the target every line of this car was drawn against.

Lando Norris and George Russell join McLaren in a year the team has framed internally as a reset, with a new car that carries none of last season's architectural compromises. a 3rd-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

The Haas launch reveals a sharply reworked sidepod concept, and it is Franco Colapinto and Mike Moors who will find out whether the theory translates on track. Measuring the ambition of this launch against a 1st-place finish last year, the direction of travel is unmistakable.

Every team in this group believes it can win; which of them is right is a question only the 2031 championship can answer.

---

### The midfield pack reveals its 2031 weapons

*Launch · Pre-season*

_Cadillac leads 4 midfield teams into the open, each convinced its winter work has found time in the middle of the pack._

The most unpredictable part of the grid is in the open. 4 midfield teams have launched their 2031 machines, knowing the gaps between them will shift almost every fortnight.

The Cadillac launch reveals a sharply reworked sidepod concept, and it is Esteban Ocon and Gabriel Bortoleto who will find out whether the theory translates on track. With a 7th-place result as the honest yardstick, the 2031 car has been engineered to address every shortcoming that produced it.

Ferrari pull the covers back on a car that the design team insists solves the rear-stability issues that cost points on the high-speed circuits last year. a 4th-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

Shaped by a winter of wind-tunnel hours and a factory culture that reportedly refused to carry a single compromise into 2031, Mercedes' new car makes its case on aesthetics alone. That a 5th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

What Williams reveal today is a car that the engineers say reflects a conscious decision to chase peak downforce rather than a broad operating window. The lessons of an 8th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

The 2031 midfield story will be written over upgrade cycles, not at the launch pad.

---

### Ground-up effort on display as backmarker teams launch for 2031

*Launch · Pre-season*

_Alpine heads a group of 4 teams who launched with honest appraisals of the distance they need to travel in 2031._

Alpine and the other teams starting the 2031 season from the back of the expected order have now committed their designs to the scrutiny of the paddock. The cars reveal how each of them has chosen to prioritise their limited resources.

What Alpine reveal today is a car that the engineers say reflects a conscious decision to chase peak downforce rather than a broad operating window. Engineers were handed a 6th-place finish as their starting point and asked to find time everywhere.

Lewis Hamilton and Dion Gunther stepped into the simulator for the first time last month and the feedback, by all accounts, was encouraging for everyone at Racing Bulls. a 9th-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

Shaped by a winter of wind-tunnel hours and a factory culture that reportedly refused to carry a single compromise into 2031, Aston Martin's new car makes its case on aesthetics alone. Last year's 11th-place constructors standing sent the drawing office back to first principles over the winter.

Every aero surface on Audi's 2031 contender is new, and Isack Hadjar and Sebastian Lehner have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. The lessons of a 10th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

The gap to the pack ahead has been the winter's primary brief; whether the answers found are sufficient will take a full season to measure.

### Verstappen moves clear after the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 24*

_Max Verstappen claimed his 4th win of the season at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix._

It was Verstappen who mastered the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix, in command whenever it mattered. Verstappen had 4.8s in hand over Norris at the chequered flag, Charles Leclerc in third. It was 25 points banked for Verstappen and Red Bull.

Moors took pole but found no answer to Verstappen once the race was underway. Moors had grabbed pole by 0.047s from Verstappen. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Verstappen found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 5 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Esteban Ocon, Pierre Gasly, Carlos Sainz, Liam Lawson and Oliver Bearman all failed to see the flag.

A scruffy pit stop briefly set nerves jangling on the Red Bull wall before the lead was safe again.

In the championship, Mike Moors stays in front, 41 points clear of Franco Colapinto.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Verstappen.

### Colapinto triumphs at the Qatar Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 23*

_A composed afternoon from Colapinto puts Haas on the top step at the Qatar Grand Prix._

Franco Colapinto controlled the Qatar Grand Prix from the front, untroubled once he had the lead. The gap to Mike Moors at the flag was 2.7s, Max Verstappen rounding out the podium behind. A maximum 25-point haul was the reward for the drive.

Moors led the early laps only for Colapinto to find a way ahead as the strategy unfolded. Moors had grabbed pole by 0.004s from Norris. Colapinto executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

Only Arvid Lindblad failed to make the flag, a hydraulics leak the cause.

There were tired but satisfied faces all through the Haas engineering room.

The arithmetic is settled, Mike Moors now champion with 37 points in hand and 1 race remaining.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Colapinto.

### The Alpine garage is becoming one-sided

*Analysis · Round 23*

_Oscar Piastri leads Leonard Grube 68 to 14 at Alpine._

Oscar Piastri leads Leonard Grube 68 to 14, a 54-point gap that has opened up inside the same garage. The 54 points do not merely represent races lost; they represent constructor points that Alpine are only half-claiming from their budget.

The recent trend is grim for Grube, beaten 0 to 14 on points over the last 3 races.

For Grube, the most damaging consequence is not the points gap itself but the internal leverage it hands to Piastri when engineering resources are allocated. The question for Grube is whether the gap gets addressed through performance or rationalised through excuses, and the paddock is watching for which answer emerges.

From Piastri's perspective the pattern is straightforward: translate car pace into points more efficiently than Grube, round after round. Until Grube can outscore Piastri on consecutive weekends, the gap will remain the story inside the Alpine garage.

### The rumours reshaping the grid with the season nearly over

*Silly season · Round 23*

_The market is moving with the season nearly over, and 4 moves are at the centre of it._

The paddock grapevine rarely quiets with the season nearly over, and right now 4 moves are demanding attention.

Sources inside Haas describe Max Verstappen as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Red Bull are 2nd, Haas are 1st. The ledger reads 315 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Haas see Verstappen's racecraft and composure as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Verstappen here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Verstappen in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Speculation over a Mike Moors departure from Haas to Ferrari has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Haas in 1st, Ferrari in 4th. A tally of 372 points has done the reputation no harm. What Ferrari are buying, if this goes through, is Moors' all-round package, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Moors room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

A move linking Esteban Ocon with Red Bull has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 7th at Cadillac to 2nd at Red Bull. Ocon's 35 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Red Bull, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Ocon's deal expiring, Cadillac face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

Pierre Gasly has emerged as a target for Aston Martin. Strange optics attach to this one: Racing Bulls sit 9th, Aston Martin sit 11th. The attraction for Aston Martin is clear: Gasly would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Gasly reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Paddock talk is cheap, but it rarely emerges from nothing; at least some of this will land.

### Red Bull claim the Las Vegas Grand Prix through Verstappen

*Race report · Round 22*

_Max Verstappen claimed his 3rd win of the season at the Las Vegas Grand Prix._

A commanding drive carried Max Verstappen to victory at the Las Vegas Grand Prix for Red Bull. Verstappen had 16.0s in hand over Norris at the chequered flag, Charles Leclerc in third. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Verstappen took every one of them.

The front row had Norris on top, yet it was Verstappen who held the advantage when it mattered. Qualifying had gone to Norris by 0.029s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. Nobody gained more ground than Max Verstappen, 5 places from 6th to 1st in a drive that caught the eye. The win was built on a one-stop strategy, with Verstappen making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

4 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Gasly with a hydraulics leak, Moors with a gearbox problem, Larson with an electrical failure and Schuster with a wheel-nut problem at a stop.

Verstappen kept the visor down through most of the slow-down lap, spent after a hard afternoon.

Atop the standings, Mike Moors keeps a 44-point cushion over Franco Colapinto.

"Getting through the first few corners cleanly let me build the gap rather than defend," said Verstappen.

Charles Leclerc produced the drive of the afternoon, charging from 6th into the points in 1st. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Leclerc found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 2 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Arvid Lindblad and Lando Norris both failed to see the flag.

In the championship, Mike Moors stays in front, 54 points clear of Franco Colapinto.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Leclerc.

### Victory for Russell at the Singapore Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 18*

_McLaren top the podium at the Singapore Grand Prix as Russell holds off Moors._

George Russell won the Singapore Grand Prix, converting pace into points on a day that belonged to McLaren. Russell finished 5.7s clear of Mike Moors, with Max Verstappen a further step back in third. A maximum 25-point haul was the reward for the drive.

Moors led the early laps only for Russell to find a way ahead as the strategy unfolded. Moors had grabbed pole by 0.007s from Colapinto. Best-mover honours went to Leonard Grube, who climbed from 14th to 9th over the course of the race. McLaren committed to a two-stop strategy from the outset and Russell drove it to perfection.

4 cars did not make the finish, thinning the points-paying places as the race wore on. Out of the running were Colapinto with an engine that let go, Lehner with a power-unit failure, Larson with a wheel-nut problem at a stop and Schuster with damage from a first-lap clash.

Russell held the trophy in both hands and looked out into the crowd before the formalities resumed.

Mike Moors holds the points lead on 296, 47 points up on Franco Colapinto.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Russell.

### Red-hot Max Verstappen hits form

*Analysis · Round 18*

_Max Verstappen is the form pick of the grid._

Max Verstappen has delivered a 3rd place and two 1st places across the last three rounds, a sequence that places Verstappen among the outstanding performers on the current grid. Three consecutive high finishes compound in the standings in ways that single strong races do not; Verstappen has effectively banked a championship buffer during this run.

Verstappen is at a stage of form where car reads are sharp, tyre decisions are costing less and race management leaves rivals short of opportunity. The most dangerous form in racing is the kind built on reliability rather than luck, and Verstappen's recent run has that quality.

Red Bull are pulling more points from the constructors' pot than their car's pace tier would ordinarily suggest, and Verstappen's run is the primary reason. The next test is a circuit that may not suit Verstappen's natural strengths, and how Verstappen adapts will say something about the depth of this form.

"I feel completely at one with the car," Verstappen said.

### The rumours reshaping the grid as the campaign enters its closing stretch

*Silly season · Round 18*

_Moors heads a list of 5 moves worth following as the campaign enters its closing stretch._

Silly season rarely waits for an invitation, and as the campaign enters its closing stretch it has arrived in full: 5 moves are in genuine circulation.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Mike Moors as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 1st to 3rd, which demands explanation. The ledger reads 296 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Red Bull see Moors' fresh edge as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Moors here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Moors in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Speculation over a Max Verstappen departure from Red Bull to Haas has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Red Bull currently sit 3rd while Haas occupy 1st. A tally of 233 points has done the reputation no harm. What Haas are buying, if this goes through, is Verstappen's racecraft and composure, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Verstappen room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Verstappen said, holding the line.

Esteban Ocon has emerged as a target for Ferrari. The gap between 7th and 4th in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. The attraction for Ferrari is clear: Ocon would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Ocon reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

A move linking Liam Lawson with Alpine has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Alpine are 6th, behind Ferrari in 4th. Lawson's 140 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Alpine, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Lawson's deal expiring, Ferrari face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Lawson acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

A move linking Pierre Gasly with Cadillac has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 9th at Racing Bulls to 7th at Cadillac. Inside Cadillac, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Gasly's deal expiring, Racing Bulls face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

None of it is signed, and a single strong result, or a poor one, can unseat what looked settled.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Verstappen.

### Silly season finds its legs at the midway point of the season

*Silly season · Round 12*

_7 moves are commanding attention in the paddock at the midway point of the season._

The paddock grapevine rarely quiets at the midway point of the season, and right now 7 moves are demanding attention.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Oscar Piastri as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Alpine are 6th, Red Bull are 3rd. The ledger reads 32 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Red Bull see Piastri's all-round package as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Piastri here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

Speculation over a Mike Moors departure from Haas to Ferrari has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Haas in 1st, Ferrari in 4th. A tally of 214 points has done the reputation no harm. What Ferrari are buying, if this goes through, is Moors' all-round package, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Moors room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Speculation over a Kimi Antonelli departure from Mercedes to Alpine has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Mercedes in 5th, Alpine in 6th. A tally of 31 points has done the reputation no harm. What Alpine are buying, if this goes through, is Antonelli's all-round package, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Antonelli room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Sources inside Haas describe Max Verstappen as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Red Bull are 3rd, Haas are 1st. The ledger reads 134 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Haas see Verstappen's big-race temperament as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Verstappen here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Verstappen in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Liam Lawson has emerged as a target for Cadillac. Strange optics attach to this one: Ferrari sit 4th, Cadillac sit 7th. Put plainly, 90 points is the number that has put Lawson on shortlists. The attraction for Cadillac is clear: Lawson would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Lawson reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

A move linking Esteban Ocon with Mercedes has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 7th at Cadillac to 5th at Mercedes. Inside Mercedes, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Ocon's deal expiring, Cadillac face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

Sources inside Cadillac describe Lewis Hamilton as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Aston Martin are 11th, Cadillac are 7th. Cadillac see Hamilton's all-round package as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Hamilton here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

Paddock talk is cheap, but it rarely emerges from nothing; at least some of this will land.

### Mike Moors wins the British Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 11*

_Mike Moors won the British Grand Prix, finishing 2.5s clear of Lando Norris._

Mike Moors saw off the field at the British Grand Prix, never letting the race slip from his grasp. The gap to Lando Norris at the flag was 2.5s, Charles Leclerc rounding out the podium behind. There was nothing left on the table, 25 points and the win for Moors.

The front row had Colapinto on top, yet it was Moors who held the advantage when it mattered. Qualifying had gone to Colapinto by 0.120s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Moors found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 2 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Leonidas Schuster and Max Verstappen both failed to see the flag.

The body language in the Cadillac garage told the story of a race that slipped away from Schuster.

In the championship, Franco Colapinto stays in front, 6 points clear of Mike Moors.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Moors.

Colapinto set the pace in qualifying, but Norris reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Colapinto by 0.072s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. McLaren committed to a one-stop strategy from the outset and Norris drove it to perfection.

### Colapinto converts pace into victory at the Barcelona-Catalunya Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 9*

_Haas top the podium at the Barcelona-Catalunya Grand Prix as Colapinto holds off Verstappen._

There was a composed inevitability to Colapinto's win at the Barcelona-Catalunya Grand Prix. Colapinto finished 4.9s clear of Max Verstappen, with George Russell a further step back in third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Haas.

Moors led the early laps only for Colapinto to find a way ahead as the strategy unfolded. Moors had grabbed pole by 0.006s from Verstappen. Carlos Sainz produced the drive of the afternoon, charging from 15th into the points in 10th. Colapinto executed a two-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The retirement count reached 6 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Schuster with a gearbox problem, Antonelli with brake failure, Lehner with a power-unit failure, Hamilton with an engine that let go, Lindblad with a hydraulics leak and Ocon with an oil leak.

Colapinto stood on the podium with the look of someone who knew the result had been earned, not gifted.

The points lead changes hands, Franco Colapinto now in front of Mike Moors by 4 points.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Colapinto.

3 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Russell with an engine that let go, Sainz with a power-unit failure and Lehner with terminal floor damage.

Russell sat quietly for a while before facing anyone.

Atop the standings, Mike Moors keeps an 8-point cushion over Franco Colapinto.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Moors.

### Haas celebrate as Colapinto takes Chinese Grand Prix honours

### Russell converts pace into victory at the Australian Grand Prix

Norris set the pace in qualifying, but Russell reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Norris by 0.022s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. There were tired but satisfied faces all through the McLaren engineering room.

"These points matter. Every race this season has felt like it counts, and today was no different," said Russell.

### Haas and McLaren draw first blood in 2030

Pace advantage is a starting position in a championship, not a finishing one, and the history of the sport is built on teams who led pre-season tests and then watched rivals close the gap round by round.

### Title contenders show their hand for 2030

*Launch · Pre-season*

_3 teams, Haas among them, have laid out cars they expect to see at the front in 2030._

Pre-season proper is underway as 3 front-running teams bring their 2030 machines into the open. Haas arrives with the loudest statement, but the rest of the group have not come to make up the numbers.

Compact, low, and visually striking, Haas' launch car makes a statement before Franco Colapinto and Mike Moors have turned a wheel in anger. The lessons of a 3rd-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

Lando Norris and George Russell stepped into the simulator for the first time last month and the feedback, by all accounts, was encouraging for everyone at McLaren. Closing the chapter on a 1st-place campaign, this car represents the team's answer to the questions that season raised.

At Ferrari, Liam Lawson and Sergio Larson debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. Engineers were handed a 4th-place finish as their starting point and asked to find time everywhere.

The pace claims will be tested soon enough, and no launch rendering has ever won a points haul.

---

### Upgrade season starts at launch as midfield teams unveil for 2030

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The midfield is rarely decided at the launch, but 4 teams, Red Bull among them, have given the first clues._

The most unpredictable part of the grid is in the open. 4 midfield teams have launched their 2030 machines, knowing the gaps between them will shift almost every fortnight.

Over at Red Bull, Charles Leclerc and Max Verstappen will carry the hopes of a factory that spent the winter rethinking its aerodynamic philosophy from the floor up. With a 2nd-place result as the honest yardstick, the 2030 car has been engineered to address every shortcoming that produced it.

Every aero surface on Cadillac's 2030 contender is new, and Esteban Ocon and Leonidas Schuster have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. Engineers were handed a 7th-place finish as their starting point and asked to find time everywhere.

At Mercedes, Kimi Antonelli and Oliver Bearman debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. The lessons of a 5th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

Oscar Piastri and Leonard Grube stepped into the simulator for the first time last month and the feedback, by all accounts, was encouraging for everyone at Alpine. That a 6th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

The 2030 midfield story will be written over upgrade cycles, not at the launch pad.

---

### The back of the grid shows its 2030 ambitions

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The 2030 grid is complete at its back end as 4 teams launch cars built to close the gap to the pack ahead._

The 2030 grid is filled in at its rear end as 4 teams bring their machines into public view. The challenge ahead of each of them is documented and significant, but the launches reveal teams that are working methodically toward the pack.

Over at Aston Martin, Lewis Hamilton and Lance Stroll will carry the hopes of a factory that spent the winter rethinking its aerodynamic philosophy from the floor up. Engineers were handed an 11th-place finish as their starting point and asked to find time everywhere.

Compact, low, and visually striking, Williams' launch car makes a statement before Carlos Sainz and Arvid Lindblad have turned a wheel in anger. The design brief was clear: improve on a 9th-place constructors result that left the factory hungry for more.

At Racing Bulls, Pierre Gasly and Alexander Albon debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. Measuring the ambition of this launch against an 8th-place finish last year, the direction of travel is unmistakable.

Isack Hadjar and Sebastian Lehner are tasked with extracting the maximum from an Audi package that engineers describe as the most cohesive they have produced in years. Last year's 10th-place constructors standing sent the drawing office back to first principles over the winter.

Progress in this part of the grid is measured in tenths chipped away over a full season, and the teams here know it.

### The Ferrari garage is becoming one-sided

*Analysis · Round 23*

_Liam Lawson has work to do against Isack Hadjar._

A 28-point margin separates Isack Hadjar from Liam Lawson at Ferrari, the widest the intra-team gulf has been this season. A gap of that magnitude between teammates is not noise; it reflects a consistent edge in race execution and qualifying trim.

The recent trend is grim for Lawson, beaten 32 to 48 on points over the last 3 races.

A trailing teammate rarely faces pressure from outside the car alone; the data that lands on the engineer's desk every Sunday evening tells its own story at Ferrari. Every race weekend Lawson fails to close the gap, the burden of expectation compounds.

The internal pecking order at Ferrari is hardening into something that will be difficult for Lawson to overturn without a clear step forward in raw qualifying pace. A single strong weekend can shift the narrative, but Lawson needs a string of them to dent a gap this wide.

"The results do not reflect the effort," Lawson said.

### Silly season finds its legs with the season nearly over

*Silly season · Round 23*

_Hadjar heads a list of 4 moves worth following with the season nearly over._

Silly season rarely waits for an invitation, and with the season nearly over it has arrived in full: 4 moves are in genuine circulation.

A move linking Isack Hadjar with McLaren has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 4th at Ferrari to 1st at McLaren. Hadjar's 205 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside McLaren, the argument centres on a proven race winner, something the current roster cannot supply. With Hadjar's deal expiring, Ferrari face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

A move linking Carlos Sainz with Cadillac has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 8th at Williams to 7th at Cadillac. Inside Cadillac, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Sainz's deal expiring, Williams face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

The timing matters: Ocon reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

A move linking George Russell with Ferrari has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Ferrari are 4th, behind McLaren in 1st. Russell's 373 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Ferrari, the argument centres on a proven race winner, something the current roster cannot supply. With Russell's deal expiring, McLaren face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

### George Russell wins the Las Vegas Grand Prix

Russell made the most of pole, getting away cleanly and building a gap before the first stops. Russell had grabbed pole by 0.056s from Norris. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Russell found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 3 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Lando Norris, Charles Leclerc and Franco Colapinto all failed to see the flag.

A scruffy pit stop briefly set nerves jangling on the McLaren wall before the lead was safe again.

With the win, George Russell can no longer be caught in the championship.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Russell.

### The seats drawing attention at the midway point of the season

*Silly season · Round 12*

_The market is moving at the midway point of the season, and 3 moves are at the centre of it._

Silly season rarely waits for an invitation, and at the midway point of the season it has arrived in full: 3 moves are in genuine circulation.

Esteban Ocon has emerged as a target for Ferrari. The gap between 7th and 4th in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. The attraction for Ferrari is clear: Ocon would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Ocon reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

A move linking Isack Hadjar with Racing Bulls has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Racing Bulls are 8th, behind Ferrari in 4th. Hadjar's 77 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Racing Bulls, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Hadjar's deal expiring, Ferrari face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Hadjar acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

Sources inside Cadillac describe Alexander Albon as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Racing Bulls are 8th, Cadillac are 7th. Cadillac see Albon's big-race temperament as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Albon here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Albon in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

None of it is signed, and a single strong result, or a poor one, can unseat what looked settled.

Mike Moors added the Monaco Grand Prix to his record, seeing off the pressure from the pack behind. By the finish the cushion over George Russell stood at 11.7s, with Lando Norris third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Haas.

Moors converted pole the efficient way, never relinquishing the lead and managing the gap as required. Moors had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.048s. Nobody gained more ground than Oliver Bearman, 6 places from 10th to 4th in a drive that caught the eye. Running a two-stop strategy on mediums, Moors found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 5 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Leonidas Schuster, Franco Colapinto, Charles Leclerc, Max Verstappen and Isack Hadjar all failed to see the flag.

The body language in the Cadillac garage told the story of a race that slipped away from Schuster.

In the championship, Mike Moors stays in front, 30 points clear of George Russell.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Moors.

### Franco Colapinto struggling in the Haas fight

### Haas and McLaren draw first blood in 2029

*Preview · Pre-season*

_Before a points-paying lap is turned, Haas have already made their intentions plain with the quickest car in the paddock._

Reliability is the silent variable that reshapes title fights, and a car carrying the lap time Haas have shown inevitably carries the complexity that brings risk alongside speed.

### Title contenders show their hand for 2029

*Launch · Pre-season*

_From Haas to the back of this elite pack, 3 teams believe they have the tools to challenge for wins in 2029._

Scrutiny falls on 3 teams as the fastest expected cars of 2029 make their public debut. Haas may lead the conversation, but the entire group has arrived with something to say.

Franco Colapinto and Mike Moors stepped into the simulator for the first time last month and the feedback, by all accounts, was encouraging for everyone at Haas. a 1st-place finish last season set the target every line of this car was drawn against.

A leaner rear packaging concept anchors McLaren's 2029 challenger, with Lando Norris and George Russell set to discover whether the trade-offs are worth it when the lights go out in testing. That a 2nd-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

What Red Bull reveal today is a car that the engineers say reflects a conscious decision to chase peak downforce rather than a broad operating window. a 3rd-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

The launches confirm intent; only laps will confirm whether the engineering has delivered on the winter's promises.

---

### Upgrade season starts at launch as midfield teams unveil for 2029

*Launch · Pre-season*

_Ferrari leads 4 midfield teams into the open, each convinced its winter work has found time in the middle of the pack._

The most unpredictable part of the grid is in the open. 4 midfield teams have launched their 2029 machines, knowing the gaps between them will shift almost every fortnight.

Built around a revised suspension geometry that the Ferrari technical staff have been pushing for two seasons, the 2029 car hands Isack Hadjar and Liam Lawson a notably different tool. The 8th-place finish that closed out last season is the number the whole factory has been trying to make obsolete.

The factory mood at Mercedes has been quietly confident all winter, and the machine Kimi Antonelli and Oliver Bearman are set to race suggests there is substance behind that confidence. The lessons of a 5th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

From the nose cone to the diffuser, Alpine have gone again, and Oscar Piastri and Leonard Grube arrive at the launch with a reputation to build on. From a 4th-place championship position, the team's stated aim is to move the needle decisively in 2029.

At Cadillac, Esteban Ocon and Leonidas Schuster debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. Last year's 7th-place constructors standing sent the drawing office back to first principles over the winter.

Whatever advantage exists between them today will likely be gone, reversed, and rebuilt before the 2029 title is decided.

---

### Ambitious launches as the back of the grid builds toward 2029

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The 2029 grid is complete at its back end as 4 teams launch cars built to close the gap to the pack ahead._

Not every car in 2029 will fight for points from round one, but the teams at the back of the grid have not come without a plan. 4 teams have now launched, each with a development arc that extends well beyond the opening race.

The factory mood at Williams has been quietly confident all winter, and the machine Carlos Sainz and Arvid Lindblad are set to race suggests there is substance behind that confidence. From a 9th-place championship position, the team's stated aim is to move the needle decisively in 2029.

Pierre Gasly and Alexander Albon are tasked with extracting the maximum from a Racing Bulls package that engineers describe as the most cohesive they have produced in years. a 6th-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

A leaner rear packaging concept anchors Aston Martin's 2029 challenger, with Lewis Hamilton and Lance Stroll set to discover whether the trade-offs are worth it when the lights go out in testing. The lessons of an 11th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

From the nose cone to the diffuser, Audi have gone again, and Gabriel Bortoleto and Sebastian Lehner arrive at the launch with a reputation to build on. That a 10th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

Launches here are declarations of direction more than declarations of pace, and the direction from each of these teams is forward.

### Ferrari leaving points on the table

*Analysis · Round 24*

_Ferrari are not making their pace count._

By pace metrics, Ferrari operate a midfield machine; by the actual results column, they sit 8th, a position no midfield car should occupy at this stage of the campaign. The squandered pace is not a marginal figure; it translates directly into prize-fund distribution, circuit leverage and the development runway that determines where the team sits in twelve months.

Ferrari's car pace means they enter most race weekends as a higher-points threat than their tally reflects; the conversion failure is a process and decision-making problem, not a technical one. The gap between what their car can score and what it is scoring represents a quantifiable management failure that the Ferrari leadership is now under public pressure to address.

A midfield car trapped 8th in the standings is a resource allocation problem as much as a sporting one: the prize money differential between 8th and where the car belongs is not trivial. What Ferrari need is not a new car but a new discipline around the decisions that convert fast machinery into actual championship points.

### McLaren claim the Qatar Grand Prix through Norris

*Race report · Round 23*

_Lando Norris claimed his 4th win of the season at the Qatar Grand Prix._

A commanding drive carried Lando Norris to victory at the Qatar Grand Prix for McLaren. By the finish the cushion over George Russell stood at 7.0s, with Oscar Piastri third. It was 25 points banked for Norris and McLaren.

Russell used the grid advantage early, yet Norris had the measure of the race overall. Russell had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.035s. The standout recovery came from Oscar Piastri, who advanced 4 places to finish 3rd. The win was built on a one-stop strategy, with Norris making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

2 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Hadjar with an engine that let go and Sainz with a high-speed spin into the barriers.

Mike Moors has effectively wrapped up the championship, 46 points clear with 1 race to run.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Norris.

### The seats drawing attention with the season nearly over

*Silly season · Round 23*

_6 moves are commanding attention in the paddock with the season nearly over._

The market has teeth with the season nearly over, and Esteban Ocon is the name that keeps surfacing above the rest.

Speculation over an Esteban Ocon departure from Racing Bulls to Red Bull has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Racing Bulls currently sit 6th while Red Bull occupy 3rd. A tally of 41 points has done the reputation no harm. What Red Bull are buying, if this goes through, is Ocon's racecraft and composure, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Ocon room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Max Verstappen has emerged as a target for Alpine. Strange optics attach to this one: Red Bull sit 3rd, Alpine sit 4th. Put plainly, 230 points is the number that has put Verstappen on shortlists. The attraction for Alpine is clear: Verstappen would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Verstappen reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Sources inside Ferrari describe Pierre Gasly as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 4th to 8th, which demands explanation. The ledger reads 129 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Ferrari see Gasly's racecraft and composure as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Gasly here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Gasly in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Speculation over a Liam Lawson departure from Ferrari to Cadillac has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Ferrari currently sit 8th while Cadillac occupy 7th. What Cadillac are buying, if this goes through, is Lawson's fresh edge, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Lawson room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Sources inside Ferrari describe Leonard Grube as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 7th to 8th, which demands explanation. Ferrari see Grube's fresh edge as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Grube here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Grube in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Lewis Hamilton has emerged as a target for Racing Bulls. The gap between 7th and 6th in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. The attraction for Racing Bulls is clear: Hamilton would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Hamilton reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds. "At this point in the season my head is completely on the job here," Hamilton said, smiling through the question.

The rumour mill turns faster than the contract pen, and plenty will change before ink dries.

### Moors triumphs at the Las Vegas Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 22*

_A composed afternoon from Moors puts Haas on the top step at the Las Vegas Grand Prix._

Mike Moors controlled the Las Vegas Grand Prix from the front, untroubled once he had the lead. Moors finished 17.4s clear of George Russell, with Franco Colapinto a further step back in third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Haas.

Norris led the early laps only for Moors to find a way ahead as the strategy unfolded. Norris had grabbed pole by 0.024s from Russell. Moors executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

Only Charles Leclerc failed to make the flag, terminal floor damage the cause.

The Haas garage erupted as Moors crossed the line, months of work landing in a single moment.

The arithmetic is settled, Mike Moors now champion with 52 points in hand and 2 races remaining.

### The rumours reshaping the grid as the campaign enters its closing stretch

*Silly season · Round 18*

_Ocon heads a list of 4 moves worth following as the campaign enters its closing stretch._

Driver talk has sharpened as the campaign enters its closing stretch, with 4 moves circulating and Esteban Ocon attracting the loudest noise.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Esteban Ocon as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Racing Bulls are 6th, Red Bull are 3rd. The ledger reads 40 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Red Bull see Ocon's marketability as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Ocon here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Ocon in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Sources inside Racing Bulls describe Liam Lawson as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Ferrari are 8th, Racing Bulls are 6th. Racing Bulls see Lawson's marketability as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Lawson here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

A move linking Lewis Hamilton with Ferrari has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Ferrari are 8th, behind Cadillac in 7th. Inside Ferrari, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Hamilton's deal expiring, Cadillac face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

Speculation over a Leonard Grube departure from Cadillac to Racing Bulls has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Cadillac currently sit 7th while Racing Bulls occupy 6th. What Racing Bulls are buying, if this goes through, is Grube's fresh edge, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Grube room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Everything here is smoke for now, though silly season has a reliable habit of producing fire.

### George Russell can do no wrong

*Silly season · Round 12*

_The market is moving at the midway point of the season, and 3 moves are at the centre of it._

The market has teeth at the midway point of the season, and Max Verstappen is the name that keeps surfacing above the rest.

Max Verstappen has emerged as a target for Alpine. Strange optics attach to this one: Red Bull sit 3rd, Alpine sit 4th. Put plainly, 125 points is the number that has put Verstappen on shortlists. The attraction for Alpine is clear: Verstappen would add a known quantity to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Verstappen reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds. "At this point in the season my head is completely on the job here," Verstappen said, smiling through the question.

Sources inside Ferrari describe Pierre Gasly as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 4th to 8th, which demands explanation. The ledger reads 94 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Ferrari see Gasly's big-race temperament as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Gasly here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Leonard Grube as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Cadillac are 7th, Red Bull are 3rd. Red Bull see Grube's fearlessness as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Grube here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Grube in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

The rumour mill turns faster than the contract pen, and plenty will change before ink dries.

### George Russell masters the British Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 11*

_George Russell claimed his 3rd win of the season at the British Grand Prix._

George Russell delivered at the British Grand Prix, holding the lead through the phases that decide races. Max Verstappen crossed the line 2.4s adrift, Mike Moors completing the top three. Winning in front of a home crowd added real significance to a commanding afternoon. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Russell took every one of them.

Russell led from the front after taking pole, controlling the tempo and never inviting a challenge. Russell had grabbed pole by 0.089s from Norris. Best-mover honours went to Pierre Gasly, who climbed from 10th to 5th over the course of the race. McLaren committed to a one-stop strategy from the outset and Russell drove it to perfection.

Franco Colapinto was the only retirement, out after 32 laps with a hydraulics leak.

Mike Moors holds the points lead on 167, 18 points up on George Russell.

### The Mercedes garage is becoming one-sided

*Analysis · Round 10*

_Kimi Antonelli leads Oliver Bearman 54 to 13 at Mercedes._

Kimi Antonelli and Oliver Bearman share a pit wall and an engineering group, yet the scoreboard shows Kimi Antonelli on 54 against Bearman's 13. In a points system where a single position swing is worth four points, a 41-point chasm represents multiple race weekends of compounded advantage.

The recent trend is grim for Bearman, beaten 4 to 24 on points over the last 3 races.

A trailing teammate rarely faces pressure from outside the car alone; the data that lands on the engineer's desk every Sunday evening tells its own story at Mercedes. Every race weekend Bearman fails to close the gap, the burden of expectation compounds.

Antonelli has demonstrated the capacity to extract from this car what is available; the issue is that Bearman has not matched that benchmark. Bearman must identify whether the deficit is mechanical setup, tyre management, or racecraft, because the fix differs in each case.

"My side of the garage will come good," said Bearman.

Norris kept the visor down through most of the slow-down lap, spent after a hard afternoon.

Atop the standings, Mike Moors keeps a 22-point cushion over Charles Leclerc.

### Leclerc wins for the first time at the Bahrain Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 4*

_Charles Leclerc crossed the line first at the Bahrain Grand Prix to become a Formula 1 race winner for the first time._

Charles Leclerc took the chequered flag at the Bahrain Grand Prix to register the first win of his Formula 1 career, a result that felt both earned and overdue in Red Bull's camp by the time the barriers came into view on the final lap.

Having finished runner-up 1 time before, Leclerc finally made the step to the top at the Bahrain Grand Prix, one place further than he had ever managed.

A maiden win is a threshold crossed only once; Leclerc crossed it at the Bahrain Grand Prix, and the sport recorded it there and then.

On the slow-down lap, Leclerc managed only a cracked "thank you, every single one of you" before the radio went quiet for a moment neither side wanted to fill.

"This one means everything, to me and to every single person at Red Bull who put in the work to get here," said Leclerc.

Other milestones fell at the Bahrain Grand Prix as well. Podium number 10 for Colapinto, who finished 2nd to extend a record built race by race.

---

### Lando Norris passes Kimi Antonelli to reach 2nd in career points

*Record · Round 4*

_Lando Norris now ranks 2nd all time in points after moving clear of Kimi Antonelli on 709._

The tally now stands at 709 points, enough to displace Kimi Antonelli and claim 2nd in the all-time rankings. It is another entry on a career record that has been building steadily across the seasons.

---

### 4 teams brought new parts to the Bahrain Grand Prix

*Technical · Round 4*

_The Bahrain Grand Prix doubled as a development checkpoint, with 4 teams running fresh components._

Car upgrades were a major subplot at the Bahrain Grand Prix, as 4 teams introduced fresh parts in search of a step forward.

The new components on the Mercedes, Ferrari and Cadillac car performed as intended and brought a tangible gain in race trim.

Audi found nothing from the new parts across the weekend, a frustrating return on the factory investment.

Mercedes brought the eye-catching change, a revised rear wing aimed at high-speed stability, and it delivered.

Mercedes sit 5th in the constructors' championship and will want these gains to hold as the calendar moves on.

---

### Charles Leclerc pulling clear of Max Verstappen

*Analysis · Round 4*

_The intra-team balance has tilted at Red Bull._

A 50-point margin separates Charles Leclerc from Max Verstappen at Red Bull, the widest the intra-team gulf has been this season. The 50 points do not merely represent races lost; they represent constructor points that Red Bull are only half-claiming from their budget.

And it is only widening, with Leclerc outscoring Verstappen 58 to 16 over the last 3 races.

For Verstappen, the most damaging consequence is not the points gap itself but the internal leverage it hands to Leclerc when engineering resources are allocated. Every race weekend Verstappen fails to close the gap, the burden of expectation compounds.

Leclerc has demonstrated the capacity to extract from this car what is available; the issue is that Verstappen has not matched that benchmark. Until Verstappen can outscore Leclerc on consecutive weekends, the gap will remain the story inside the Red Bull garage.

---

### Could Fernando Alonso return to the grid?

*Driver watch · Round 4*

_Out of a drive for now, Alonso is not done yet._

Fernando Alonso, 46, finds himself without a seat, a familiar face still chasing a way back.

No podiums in 24 starts, but a known quantity all the same.

"I am not done in this sport," Alonso said.

An Aston Martin seat for 2029 looks a genuine possibility.

---

### Moors sees off Leclerc to win the Japanese Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 3*

_Moors wins the Japanese Grand Prix and tightens his grip on the season._

Mike Moors was the class of the field at the Japanese Grand Prix, turning qualifying pace into race-day victory. Charles Leclerc crossed the line just 0.736s adrift, Lando Norris completing the top three. There was nothing left on the table, 25 points and the win for Moors.

Norris took pole but found no answer to Moors once the race was underway. Norris had grabbed pole by 0.134s from Moors. The win was built on a two-stop strategy, with Moors making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

2 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Lawson with a high-speed spin into the barriers and Hamilton with a power-unit failure.

Lawson sat quietly for a while before facing anyone.

Mike Moors takes over at the head of the table, 10 points ahead of George Russell.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Moors.

---

_The talking points ahead of the Japanese Grand Prix._

The grid heads to the Japanese Grand Prix for round 3. It is George Russell who tops the table, 3 points up on Mike Moors.

Moors arrives fresh off a maiden win of the season at the Chinese Grand Prix.

Moors sits 3 points behind Russell and will fancy a response. Russell carries 1 win into the weekend.

### McLaren headline a Japanese Grand Prix development push

*Technical · Round 3*

_1 team arrived at the Japanese Grand Prix with new parts, and not all of them left happy._

For McLaren, the new parts delivered, with a clear step up in competitiveness.

The new diffuser on the McLaren car was built around gains in overall downforce, and it delivered on that brief.

Sitting 1st in the standings, McLaren have given themselves fresh ammunition for the next phase of the season.

### A worrying run for Dion Gunther

*Analysis · Round 3*

_Dion Gunther has slipped down the order in recent rounds._

The last three races have brought a 20th place, a 19th place and a retirement for Dion Gunther, a run heading firmly the wrong way. Points missed in a spell like this are rarely won back, and Gunther can feel the order pulling away.

For Aston Martin, this is a compounding problem: the constructor loses points from one side of the garage at a time when development pace demands full contribution from both cars.

A back-to-basics inspection of the car is reportedly under way. Word is Gunther has been leaning on a sports psychologist.

"It will turn, I have no doubt," Gunther said.
