### Moors triumphs at the Chinese Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 2*

_Moors delivered a controlled drive to win the Chinese Grand Prix ahead of George Russell and Charles Leclerc._

Mike Moors controlled the Chinese Grand Prix from the front, untroubled once he had the lead. Moors had 17.8s in hand over Russell at the chequered flag, Charles Leclerc in third. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Moors took every one of them.

Norris set the pace in qualifying, but Moors reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Norris by 0.056s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. The standout recovery came from Lewis Hamilton, who advanced 5 places to finish 10th. Moors executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The retirement count reached 2 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Verstappen with an engine that let go and Piastri with a wheel-nut problem at a stop.

The Haas garage erupted as Moors crossed the line, months of work landing in a single moment.

No change at the top, George Russell on 43 points with Mike Moors 3 points adrift.

---

### The Chinese Grand Prix in focus

*Preview · Round 2*

_Setting the scene for the Chinese Grand Prix._

The grid heads to the Chinese Grand Prix for round 2. George Russell leads on 25 points, 7 points clear of Lando Norris.

Norris has ground to make up on Russell. Russell has 1 win to his name so far.

McLaren head the teams standings, 18 points clear of Haas.

### Moors becomes a Grand Prix winner at the Chinese Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 2*

_Mike Moors took the first win of his Formula 1 career at the Chinese Grand Prix, a result the record books now hold permanently._

The scenes in the Haas garage as Moors crossed the line told you everything about how long this had been coming; a win that felt inevitable the moment it happened and yet still stopped everyone cold.

Nobody takes a first win away from you. The Chinese Grand Prix is where Moors got his, and that fact is permanent.

Moors was surrounded in parc ferme before the car had fully stopped, mechanics clearing the barriers before protocol could slow them down.

"I have had some hard weekends in this sport, and winning for the first time makes every one of them worth it," said Moors.

The Chinese Grand Prix was a day for the history books in more ways than one. Russell, Leclerc, Colapinto, Norris, Gasly, Ocon, Antonelli, Bearman, Hamilton, Lawson, Sainz, Perez, Bortoleto, Stroll, Hadjar, Verstappen and Piastri all clocked up 50 career starts on the same afternoon.

### George Russell sets the all-time points record

*Record · Round 2*

_George Russell has passed Kimi Antonelli to become the all-time leader in points, reaching 708._

The all-time points record now belongs to George Russell, who has accumulated 708. Kimi Antonelli had held the mark on 698, a figure that stood for years before today. The record reflects a consistency and longevity that defines the very top of the sport.

### McLaren celebrate as Russell takes Australian Grand Prix honours

*Race report · Round 1*

_Russell delivered a controlled drive to win the Australian Grand Prix ahead of Lando Norris and Mike Moors._

It was Russell who mastered the Australian Grand Prix, in command whenever it mattered. By the finish the cushion over Lando Norris stood at 2.4s, with Mike Moors third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for McLaren.

The front row had Moors on top, yet it was Russell who held the advantage when it mattered. Qualifying had gone to Moors by 0.022s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. George Russell made up 5 places, climbing from 6th to a points finish in 1st. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Russell found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 2 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Isack Hadjar and Dion Gunther both failed to see the flag.

In the championship, George Russell stays in front, 7 points clear of Lando Norris.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Russell.

### A maiden rostrum for Moors at the Australian Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 1*

_The Australian Grand Prix put Moors on the rostrum for the first time, 3rd at the chequered flag._

The Australian Grand Prix is where Moors climbed to the top three for the first time; 3rd at the line, and a podium trophy that belongs to him permanently.

The fact it took a tidy, disciplined afternoon rather than a windfall makes it count for more in Haas' eyes.

"A podium in Formula 1 is something nobody can ever take from you; it is yours," said Moors.

There were other landmarks written into the record at the Australian Grand Prix. McLaren locked out the top two as well, Russell ahead of Norris. Moors qualified on pole in his first-ever pole position, the fastest through qualifying. A historic afternoon for Larson, Grube, Lehner and Gunther, each taking the start for the first time.

### Pre-season pace sets up a 2028 showdown

*Preview · Pre-season*

_Haas arrive at the first race as the team to beat, with McLaren their closest shadow on the timing screens._

Every other team on the grid is now playing catch-up, with Haas having set the pre-season bar higher than the competition was hoping to see.

The midfield is packed tightly enough that a single successful upgrade package could vault a team from sixth in the constructors' standings to third, making the chasing positions as contested as the front.

For McLaren, the task is narrowing the gap to Haas fast enough that a title fight is still mathematically alive when the calendar turns to its final third.

---

### Front-runners unveiled as 2028 pre-season begins

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The 3 teams at the sharp end of the 2028 grid are in the open, with Haas setting the early benchmark._

The cars that will contest race victories in 2028 are no longer a secret. 3 teams with credible championship ambitions have unveiled their contenders, and the engineering statements on show are striking.

From the nose cone to the diffuser, Haas have gone again, and Franco Colapinto and Mike Moors arrive at the launch with a reputation to build on. From a 1st-place championship position, the team's stated aim is to move the needle decisively in 2028.

The factory mood at McLaren has been quietly confident all winter, and the machine Lando Norris and George Russell are set to race suggests there is substance behind that confidence. Last year's 3rd-place constructors standing sent the drawing office back to first principles over the winter.

Charles Leclerc and Max Verstappen are tasked with extracting the maximum from a Red Bull package that engineers describe as the most cohesive they have produced in years. That a 5th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

The pace claims will be tested soon enough, and no launch rendering has ever won a points haul.

---

### The midfield pack reveals its 2028 weapons

*Launch · Pre-season*

_4 teams built to compete for every point on offer in 2028 have now shown what they are bringing to the fight._

Alpine and a clutch of rivals have pulled back the covers on what each of them believes is a step forward. Whether those steps are big enough to move the needle in 2028 is the question every points-chasing team is sitting with.

For Alpine, the wraps come off a machine that the drawing office has been quietly confident about since the first CFD runs landed in late autumn. The design brief was clear: improve on a 4th-place constructors result that left the factory hungry for more.

The Mercedes launch reveals a sharply reworked sidepod concept, and it is Kimi Antonelli and Oliver Bearman who will find out whether the theory translates on track. a 2nd-place finish last season set the target every line of this car was drawn against.

Every aero surface on Racing Bulls' 2028 contender is new, and Esteban Ocon and Sergio Perez have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. Last year's 7th-place constructors standing sent the drawing office back to first principles over the winter.

Built around a revised suspension geometry that the Cadillac technical staff have been pushing for two seasons, the 2028 car hands Lewis Hamilton and Leonard Grube a notably different tool. That an 11th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

In the midfield, the launch order is irrelevant; what matters is who has found the most performance when the real season begins.

---

### Ground-up effort on display as backmarker teams launch for 2028

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The 2028 grid is complete at its back end as 4 teams launch cars built to close the gap to the pack ahead._

The most honest engineering statements in any pre-season often come from the back of the grid. 4 teams have launched their 2028 cars with clear-eyed acknowledgement of the gap to close, alongside specific technical arguments for how they intend to close it.

From the nose cone to the diffuser, Ferrari have gone again, and Liam Lawson and Sergio Larson arrive at the launch with a reputation to build on. The design brief was clear: improve on a 6th-place constructors result that left the factory hungry for more.

Lance Stroll and Dion Gunther join Aston Martin in a year the team has framed internally as a reset, with a new car that carries none of last season's architectural compromises. an 8th-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

Over at Audi, Gabriel Bortoleto and Sebastian Lehner will carry the hopes of a factory that spent the winter rethinking its aerodynamic philosophy from the floor up. That a 10th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

Built around a revised suspension geometry that the Williams technical staff have been pushing for two seasons, the 2028 car hands Isack Hadjar and Carlos Sainz a notably different tool. Closing the chapter on a 9th-place campaign, this car represents the team's answer to the questions that season raised.

Launches here are declarations of direction more than declarations of pace, and the direction from each of these teams is forward.

---

### Can Lehner deliver for Audi in his debut season

*Rookie · Pre-season*

_At just 20, Sebastian Lehner joins Audi as one of the youngest drivers on the grid, carrying the weight of a junior career's worth of expectations into the harshest spotlight in motorsport._

Media commitments alone scale up sharply at Audi, with press obligations, sponsor appearances and simulator debriefs eating into the hours factory engineers want spent reviewing data.

Tyre warm-up on a cool out-lap, safety-car restarts and the call to pit or stay out are decisions Lehner rehearsed in the simulator but now executes under the full points cost of getting them wrong.

Pressure from the junior pipeline is structural and permanent, because Audi's academy produced Lehner and will produce the next candidate, making his seat conditional on performance rather than promise.

---

### Larson at 20, the rookie Ferrari are betting on

*Rookie · Pre-season*

_Formula 1 in 2028 hands Sergio Larson a seat at Ferrari, a scrutinising global audience, and no margin for a gentle learning curve._

The jump from junior categories to a full Formula 1 season compresses years of technical learning into a winter's worth of preparation, and Larson has had to process that acceleration faster than almost any rival on the 2028 grid.

Street circuits arrive without the buffer of long free-practice familiarity, replacing it with a wall on the exit of every barrier-lined chicane and a single shot at the lap.

Sponsor visibility, simulator feedback and raw lap counts will all be weighed as 2028 unfolds to justify or question Ferrari's decision to hand him a front-line seat so early.

### At the last, Haas take the 2027 constructors' crown

_Haas survive the final round to claim the 2027 constructors' championship by 4 points from Mercedes, a margin that captures a year-long fight._

From the first test to the decisive lap, Haas operated as a unit, two cars covering each other in the standings and maximising every opportunity the calendar offered.

When the points were totalled and the 4-point gap was confirmed, the relief in the Haas garage was visceral after months of work came down to one race.

### Norris moves clear after the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 24*

_Norris wins the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix and tightens his grip on the season._

Lando Norris added the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix to his record, seeing off the pressure from the pack behind. Oliver Bearman crossed the line 2.9s adrift, Pierre Gasly completing the top three. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Norris took every one of them.

Russell took pole but found no answer to Norris once the race was underway. Russell had grabbed pole by 0.067s from Norris. Running a two-stop strategy on mediums, Norris found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

The lone retirement was Kimi Antonelli, a suspension failure ending the day after 35 laps.

Norris pulled off his helmet on the slow-down lap to take in the reception from the grandstands.

In the championship, Lando Norris stays in front, 68 points clear of Oliver Bearman.

"There were moments where I had to manage it carefully, but I always felt we had something in reserve," said Norris.

The 2027 constructors championship reaches its conclusion at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix, Mercedes in front but Haas still firmly in the fight.

A 12-points buffer means Mercedes need only keep Haas within 12 points of them on Sunday to be crowned 2027 champions.

One-two at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix and it is over for Mercedes, irrespective of Haas' afternoon.

### Upgrades assessed at the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix

*Technical · Round 24*

_Upgrade season hit the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix hard, and the lap-time data has started to separate the gains from the gambles._

### Liam Lawson struggling in the Ferrari fight

*Analysis · Round 24*

_A 43-point gap inside the Ferrari garage._

Charles Leclerc leads Liam Lawson 49 to 6, a 43-point gap that has opened up inside the same garage. In a points system where a single position swing is worth four points, a 43-point chasm represents multiple race weekends of compounded advantage.

And it is only widening, with Leclerc outscoring Lawson 7 to 3 over the last 6 races.

A trailing teammate rarely faces pressure from outside the car alone; the data that lands on the engineer's desk every Sunday evening tells its own story at Ferrari. At Ferrari the number has now grown large enough that neutrals have stopped calling it a phase and started calling it a hierarchy.

From Leclerc's perspective the pattern is straightforward: translate car pace into points more efficiently than Lawson, round after round. A single strong weekend can shift the narrative, but Lawson needs a string of them to dent a gap this wide.

"My side of the garage will come good," said Lawson.

---

### Why Jonathan Reid is turning heads

### Paddock whispers grow louder with the season nearly over

*Silly season · Round 23*

_The market is moving with the season nearly over, and 7 moves are at the centre of it._

The paddock grapevine rarely quiets with the season nearly over, and right now 7 moves are demanding attention.

Lando Norris has emerged as a target for Mercedes. The gap between 3rd and 1st in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. Put plainly, 346 points is the number that has put Norris on shortlists. The attraction for Mercedes is clear: Norris would add a proven race winner to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Norris reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

A move linking Oliver Bearman with Mercedes has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 2nd at Haas to 1st at Mercedes. Bearman's 285 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Mercedes, the argument centres on a proven race winner, something the current roster cannot supply. With Bearman's deal expiring, Haas face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

Speculation over a Kimi Antonelli departure from Mercedes to McLaren has reached credible ears. It would represent a rare slide down the order: Mercedes in 1st, McLaren in 3rd. A tally of 291 points has done the reputation no harm. What McLaren are buying, if this goes through, is Antonelli's raw potential, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Antonelli room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Antonelli said, holding the line.

Speculation over a Mike Moors departure from Aston Martin to McLaren has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Aston Martin currently sit 8th while McLaren occupy 3rd. What McLaren are buying, if this goes through, is Moors' fearlessness, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Moors room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Charles Leclerc as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Ferrari are 6th, Red Bull are 5th. The ledger reads 47 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Red Bull see Leclerc's reliability between the walls as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Leclerc here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open. Put to Leclerc in the media pen, the response was a deflection dressed as an answer.

Pierre Gasly has emerged as a target for Haas. The gap between 4th and 2nd in the standings tells the story of how significant the move would be. Put plainly, 213 points is the number that has put Gasly on shortlists. The attraction for Haas is clear: Gasly would add a proven race winner to a lineup that needs it. The timing matters: Gasly reaches the end of the current deal at the season's close, which concentrates minds.

Sources inside Alpine describe Arvid Lindblad as a genuine option. Few would dispute it represents a step forward, Racing Bulls are 7th, Alpine are 4th. Alpine see Lindblad's marketability as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Lindblad here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

Paddock talk is cheap, but it rarely emerges from nothing; at least some of this will land.

Speculation over a Charles Leclerc departure from Ferrari to McLaren has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Ferrari currently sit 6th while McLaren occupy 3rd. A tally of 42 points has done the reputation no harm. What McLaren are buying, if this goes through, is Leclerc's all-round package, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Leclerc room to listen, and several teams are aware of it.

A move linking George Russell with Haas has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Haas are 2nd, behind Mercedes in 1st. Russell's 164 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Haas, the argument centres on a proven race winner, something the current roster cannot supply. With Russell's deal expiring, Mercedes face a decision, and rivals know the window is real. When the question landed, Russell acknowledged it with a shrug and gave nothing back.

Sources inside Red Bull describe Kimi Antonelli as a genuine option. The move would be a step backward by the constructors table, 1st to 5th, which demands explanation. The ledger reads 246 points, and that figure is doing the work in these conversations. Red Bull see Antonelli's fresh edge as precisely what the setup demands. Leverage sits with Antonelli here, out of contract at year's end, the choice is entirely open.

A move linking Isack Hadjar with Racing Bulls has been making the rounds for weeks. Eyebrows have been raised because Racing Bulls are 7th, behind Red Bull in 5th. Hadjar's 63 points this year provide hard evidence to underpin the interest. Inside Racing Bulls, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Hadjar's deal expiring, Red Bull face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

A move linking Carlos Sainz with Cadillac has been making the rounds for weeks. In constructors terms it is a clear promotion, 11th at Williams to 9th at Cadillac. Inside Cadillac, the argument centres on a known quantity, something the current roster cannot supply. With Sainz's deal expiring, Williams face a decision, and rivals know the window is real.

Speculation over an Arvid Lindblad departure from Racing Bulls to Ferrari has reached credible ears. The trajectory would be sharply upward: Racing Bulls currently sit 7th while Ferrari occupy 6th. What Ferrari are buying, if this goes through, is Lindblad's fresh edge, and it fits. Running down to a contract expiry gives Lindblad room to listen, and several teams are aware of it. "I have a car to focus on, that is genuinely all I can tell you," Lindblad said, holding the line.

None of it is signed, and a single strong result, or a poor one, can unseat what looked settled.

### Russell holds on for Azerbaijan Grand Prix victory

*Race report · Round 17*

_George Russell took victory at the Azerbaijan Grand Prix, with Oliver Bearman and Pierre Gasly completing the podium._

George Russell took victory at the Azerbaijan Grand Prix in a drive that answered every question put to him. Russell had 8.1s in hand over Bearman at the chequered flag, Pierre Gasly in third. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Russell took every one of them.

Norris took pole but found no answer to Russell once the race was underway. Norris had grabbed pole by 0.106s from Colapinto. Best-mover honours went to Pierre Gasly, who climbed from 8th to 3rd over the course of the race. The win was built on a one-stop strategy, with Russell making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

2 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Ocon with terminal floor damage and Hulkenberg with a suspension failure.

Ocon sat quietly for a while before facing anyone.

Atop the standings, Lando Norris keeps a 6-point cushion over Kimi Antonelli.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Russell.

### Antonelli converts pace into victory at the Spanish Grand Prix

_Kimi Antonelli claimed his 4th win of the season at the Spanish Grand Prix._

Despite Bearman starting from pole, Antonelli found the pace to overturn the deficit during the race. Bearman had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.007s. Antonelli executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The Mercedes garage erupted as Antonelli crossed the line, months of work landing in a single moment.

No change at the top, Lando Norris on 225 points with Kimi Antonelli 2 points adrift.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Antonelli.

### Colapinto moves clear after the Chinese Grand Prix

_A composed afternoon from Colapinto puts Haas on the top step at the Chinese Grand Prix._

The lone retirement was Pierre Gasly, terminal floor damage ending the day after 3 laps.

### The Chinese Grand Prix delivers Colapinto a maiden Formula 1 win

*Milestone · Round 2*

_A maiden victory for Colapinto at the Chinese Grand Prix, a moment he will carry for the rest of his career and beyond._

Franco Colapinto took the chequered flag at the Chinese Grand Prix to register the first win of his Formula 1 career, a result that felt both earned and overdue in Haas' camp by the time the barriers came into view on the final lap.

From pole, Colapinto never gave a rival a realistic look; the win was his from the first corner.

The Chinese Grand Prix will sit permanently in Colapinto's story, the place he became a Formula 1 race winner, a distinction that belongs to fewer people than most realize.

The team radio when the flag fell told you everything: a roar from the Haas wall, and Colapinto's voice cracking before the words arrived.

"I do not have the words yet; I will find them later, but right now I just want to stay in this moment," said Colapinto.

There were other landmarks written into the record at the Chinese Grand Prix. Verstappen took 3rd for Red Bull in a result that stunned the paddock. Colapinto claimed a maiden pole position, going quickest in qualifying for the first time in his career.

---

### Bearman converts pace into victory at the Australian Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 1*

_Bearman wins the Australian Grand Prix and tightens his grip on the season._

Oliver Bearman claimed the Australian Grand Prix with a controlled performance that the chasing pack could not match. Bearman had 13.2s in hand over Russell at the chequered flag, Franco Colapinto in third. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Bearman took every one of them.

Despite Russell starting from pole, Bearman found the pace to overturn the deficit during the race. Russell had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.127s. Bearman executed a two-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The retirement count reached 6 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Lawson with an electrical failure, Leclerc with terminal floor damage, Gasly with a power-unit failure, Sainz with damage from a first-lap clash, Albon with a high-speed spin into the barriers and Hamilton with an engine that let go.

Bearman stood on the podium with the look of someone who knew the result had been earned, not gifted.

No change at the top, Oliver Bearman on 25 points with George Russell 7 points adrift.

"These points matter. Every race this season has felt like it counts, and today was no different," said Bearman.

### Haas and Mercedes draw first blood in 2027

*Preview · Pre-season*

_Pre-season testing has handed Haas a clear pace advantage, putting the rest of the grid on the back foot before a race has been run._

Haas carry the fastest raw car pace into 2027, a benchmark the rest of the grid measured themselves against across every session of winter running.

Reliability is the silent variable that reshapes title fights, and a car carrying the lap time Haas have shown inevitably carries the complexity that brings risk alongside speed.

The pressure on every team outside the top two is structural, not motivational, because the resource gap between front-runners and the midfield makes genuine championship bids difficult to sustain across an entire year.

### The fastest cars of 2027 break cover

*Launch · Pre-season*

_3 teams with genuine podium ambitions have launched their 2027 contenders, led by Haas._

Haas and 3 other teams with genuine title intentions have launched their 2027 cars within days of one another, compressing the field's design philosophies into a single revealing week.

At Haas, Franco Colapinto and Oliver Bearman debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. Closing the chapter on a 7th-place campaign, this car represents the team's answer to the questions that season raised.

What Mercedes reveal today is a car that the engineers say reflects a conscious decision to chase peak downforce rather than a broad operating window. From a 1st-place championship position, the team's stated aim is to move the needle decisively in 2027.

Every aero surface on McLaren's 2027 contender is new, and Lando Norris and Lewis Hamilton have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. The lessons of a 2nd-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

How the gaps between this group actually emerge will only be known once the timing screens light up in testing.

---

### Points hunters launch as 2027 shapes up

*Launch · Pre-season*

_From Alpine to the back of the group, 4 midfield teams have launched cars that could easily swap positions by the season's end._

History says the midfield order in 2027 will look nothing like it does today, but that has not stopped 4 teams from making confident engineering statements at launch.

Compact, low, and visually striking, Alpine's launch car makes a statement before Oscar Piastri and Pierre Gasly have turned a wheel in anger. The lessons of a 5th-place season are baked into every revised package on show today.

At Red Bull, Max Verstappen and Isack Hadjar debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. a 4th-place constructors result gave the development programme a specific and uncomfortable benchmark to beat.

Ferrari pull the covers back on a car that the design team insists solves the rear-stability issues that cost points on the high-speed circuits last year. That a 3rd-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

The factory mood at Racing Bulls has been quietly confident all winter, and the machine Arvid Lindblad and Esteban Ocon are set to race suggests there is substance behind that confidence. a 6th-place finish last season set the target every line of this car was drawn against.

In the midfield, the launch order is irrelevant; what matters is who has found the most performance when the real season begins.

---

### The back of the grid shows its 2027 ambitions

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The 2027 grid is complete at its back end as 4 teams launch cars built to close the gap to the pack ahead._

Aston Martin and the other teams starting the 2027 season from the back of the expected order have now committed their designs to the scrutiny of the paddock. The cars reveal how each of them has chosen to prioritise their limited resources.

What Aston Martin reveal today is a car that the engineers say reflects a conscious decision to chase peak downforce rather than a broad operating window. Measuring the ambition of this launch against a 10th-place finish last year, the direction of travel is unmistakable.

The Audi launch reveals a sharply reworked sidepod concept, and it is Nico Hulkenberg and Gabriel Bortoleto who will find out whether the theory translates on track. That a 9th-place result was the honest baseline; what the team has built since is an attempt to move substantially beyond it.

At Cadillac, Sergio Perez and Valtteri Bottas debut a car that bears almost no visual resemblance to the chassis they ended last season with. an 11th-place finish last season set the target every line of this car was drawn against.

Williams pull the covers back on a car that the design team insists solves the rear-stability issues that cost points on the high-speed circuits last year. Closing the chapter on an 8th-place campaign, this car represents the team's answer to the questions that season raised.

How much ground these teams can recover in 2027 will depend on development pace as much as the car they launch with.

### Young gun Moors steps up for Aston Martin in 2027

_The step from junior formulae to a full Aston Martin race seat is the largest of Moors' career, and 2027 is where the world finds out whether he's ready for it._

Where the feeder series let him find rhythm over a weekend, the freight-train schedule of practice, qualifying and race demands that Moors reads a circuit and extracts the maximum before a single radio call ends.

Qualifying is the earliest and starkest test, one flying lap with no second invitation, the format that strips away context and prints a raw number beside Moors' name.

The final verdict on Moors' rookie season comes down to whether he closes the gap to his teammate across the year or lets that gap define the conversation heading into contract talks.

On the slow-down lap, Norris managed only a cracked "thank you, every single one of you" before the radio went quiet for a moment neither side wanted to fill.

### Haas underdelivering on their potential

*Analysis · Round 14*

_Haas are not making their pace count._

Haas have the raw material of a midfield car and only 8th in the constructors' standings to show for it, a conversion rate the rest of the paddock will note with interest. Formula 1 rewards pace on lap-time sheets and results on the scoreboard; Haas are proving that the two are not the same thing, to their own significant cost.

The deficit is operational: pit stop timing, undercut calls, double-stacking decisions, and the management of safety-car periods have collectively cost Haas finishing positions their car's pace had already secured. Execution under pressure is a learnable skill, but Haas have not yet shown they have learned it at the rate the 8th-place standing demands.

The longer Haas sit 8th with a car capable of better, the harder it becomes to recruit, retain, and motivate the personnel who know exactly what the car should be scoring. A clear operational review, specific accountability for the decisions that cost positions, and a measurable standard for execution are the minimum requirement for closing the gap between where they are and where their car says they should be.

3 cars did not make the finish, thinning the points-paying places as the race wore on. Out of the running were Hulkenberg with a hydraulics leak, Lindblad with terminal floor damage and Lawson with damage from a first-lap clash.

Russell held the trophy in both hands and looked out into the crowd before the formalities resumed.

George Russell holds the points lead on 258, 63 points up on Kimi Antonelli.

Whether McLaren's recent upgrade bites here is one of the weekend's questions.

The job for Antonelli is to chip into an 8-point deficit to Russell. Russell carries 2 wins into the weekend.

In the constructors, Mercedes lead Red Bull by 62 points.

Conditions around the high-speed walls of Jeddah may suit Mercedes and Kimi Antonelli.

### The Saudi Arabian Grand Prix gives Bearman a first taste of the scoreboard

*Milestone · Round 5*

_10th at the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix: Bearman has a Formula 1 points finish, and the account is open._

A first points finish is a threshold crossed only once, and Bearman crossed it with 10th at the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix, a clean afternoon that justified the belief Haas placed in him.

For Haas, seeing Bearman in the points is the return on a project that took patience, and today the patience looked worthwhile.

"This is what everyone at Haas has been working towards, and it is just the beginning," said Bearman.

### Upgrade roundup from the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix

*Technical · Round 5*

_3 teams arrived at the Saudi Arabian Grand Prix with new parts, and not all of them left happy._

The Saudi Arabian Grand Prix served as the latest proving ground for the development race, with 3 teams arriving with significant new components.

For Mercedes, Audi and Cadillac, the new parts delivered, with a clear step up in competitiveness.

The new diffuser on the Cadillac car was built around gains in low-speed balance, and it delivered on that brief.

Sitting 10th in the standings, Cadillac have given themselves fresh ammunition for the next phase of the season.

### Who is number one at Red Bull?

*Analysis · Round 5*

_Isack Hadjar has work to do against Max Verstappen._

Max Verstappen leads Isack Hadjar 75 to 34, a 41-point gap that has opened up inside the same garage. The 41 points do not merely represent races lost; they represent constructor points that Red Bull are only half-claiming from their budget.

The recent trend is grim for Hadjar, beaten 34 to 75 on points over the last 5 races.

For Hadjar, the most damaging consequence is not the points gap itself but the internal leverage it hands to Verstappen when engineering resources are allocated. The question for Hadjar is whether the gap gets addressed through performance or rationalised through excuses, and the paddock is watching for which answer emerges.

Verstappen has demonstrated the capacity to extract from this car what is available; the issue is that Hadjar has not matched that benchmark. Hadjar must identify whether the deficit is mechanical setup, tyre management, or racecraft, because the fix differs in each case.

"I am not panicking, we keep working," said Hadjar.

### Antonelli triumphs at the Bahrain Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 4*

_Kimi Antonelli took victory at the Bahrain Grand Prix, with George Russell and Lewis Hamilton completing the podium._

Kimi Antonelli controlled the Bahrain Grand Prix from the front, untroubled once he had the lead. By the finish the cushion over George Russell stood at 8.0s, with Lewis Hamilton third. It was 25 points banked for Antonelli and Mercedes.

Starting on pole, Antonelli covered the early laps precisely and put clear air between him and the pack. Qualifying had gone to Antonelli by 0.081s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. Antonelli executed a one-stop strategy, starting on mediums, and the timing of the stops proved to be the margin.

The retirement count reached 2 cars, changing the complexion of the midfield. The retirements read Colapinto with brake failure and Stroll with damage from a first-lap clash.

There were tired but satisfied faces all through the Mercedes engineering room.

No change at the top, George Russell on 76 points with Kimi Antonelli 8 points adrift.

"It is never easy until it is over, so I kept pushing every single lap," said Antonelli.

_A busy weekend in the garages as 3 teams chased performance with new parts at the Bahrain Grand Prix._

Factory work arrived at the track this weekend, with 3 teams running new components for the first time at the Bahrain Grand Prix.

For McLaren, Red Bull and Aston Martin, the new parts delivered, with a clear step up in competitiveness.

A redesigned rear wing was the centrepiece of Aston Martin's package, with the team targeting high-speed stability and finding the gains.

Aston Martin occupy 9th in the constructors' championship and now have a confirmed step to build from.

### The slump deepens for Lance Stroll

*Analysis · Round 4*

_Points have dried up for Lance Stroll._

Lance Stroll has posted a retirement, a 19th place and a 20th place in the last three races, a sequence that has dropped the Aston Martin driver well off the scoring pace. Points missed in a spell like this are rarely won back, and Stroll can feel the order pulling away.

The concerning aspect for Aston Martin is that no single obvious cause has been identified publicly, which makes the reset harder to engineer.

There is talk of a chassis change to rule out hidden damage.

"It will turn, I have no doubt," Stroll said.

### Russell moves clear after the Japanese Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 3*

_George Russell won the Japanese Grand Prix, finishing 13.0s clear of Charles Leclerc._

George Russell saw off the field at the Japanese Grand Prix, never letting the race slip from his grasp. Charles Leclerc crossed the line 13.0s adrift, Max Verstappen completing the top three. The 25 points were the maximum on offer, and Russell took every one of them.

Russell converted pole the efficient way, never relinquishing the lead and managing the gap as required. Russell had split the field in qualifying, taking pole by 0.043s. Best-mover honours went to Alexander Albon, who climbed from 16th to 9th over the course of the race. Running a one-stop strategy on mediums, Russell found the rhythm the tyres allowed and never looked back.

Attrition accounted for 3 cars before the flag, reshaping the order behind the leaders. Gabriel Bortoleto, Kimi Antonelli and Sergio Perez all failed to see the flag.

A scruffy pit stop briefly set nerves jangling on the Mercedes wall before the lead was safe again.

The result swings the championship, and George Russell now leads.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Russell.

### A maiden rostrum for Leclerc at the Japanese Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 3*

_Charles Leclerc finished 2nd at the Japanese Grand Prix to claim the first podium of his Formula 1 career._

Charles Leclerc brought Ferrari's car home 2nd at the Japanese Grand Prix to stand on a Formula 1 podium for the first time, a milestone that no subsequent result can take away.

The fact it took a tidy, disciplined afternoon rather than a windfall makes it count for more in Ferrari's eyes.

"A podium in Formula 1 is something nobody can ever take from you; it is yours," said Leclerc.

There were other landmarks written into the record at the Japanese Grand Prix. A landmark afternoon for Albon and Lawson, who all got off the mark in the championship.

### Development verdicts from the Japanese Grand Prix

*Technical · Round 3*

_Upgrade season hit the Japanese Grand Prix hard, and the lap-time data has started to separate the gains from the gambles._

Only one team came to the Japanese Grand Prix carrying new parts, making their update the story of the garage.

The new components on the Racing Bulls car performed as intended and brought a tangible gain in race trim.

Racing Bulls introduced a new front-wing endplate with overall downforce as the primary objective, and the data backed up the concept.

Racing Bulls are 8th in the constructors' standings, and a working upgrade puts them in a stronger position to push higher.

*Analysis · Round 3*

_The form guide makes grim reading for Gabriel Bortoleto._

The last three races have brought two retirements and a 13th place for Gabriel Bortoleto, a run heading firmly the wrong way. That run has cost Bortoleto ground just as the rest of the field keeps banking finishes.

For Audi, this is a compounding problem: the constructor loses points from one side of the garage at a time when development pace demands full contribution from both cars.

### George Russell masters the Chinese Grand Prix

*Race report · Round 2*

_George Russell won the Chinese Grand Prix, finishing 24.9s clear of Kimi Antonelli._

George Russell won the Chinese Grand Prix, converting pace into points on a day that belonged to Mercedes. By the finish the cushion over Kimi Antonelli stood at 24.9s, with Max Verstappen third. The full 25 points capped a near-flawless weekend for Mercedes.

Antonelli set the pace in qualifying, but Russell reversed the order when it counted on Sunday. Qualifying had gone to Antonelli by 0.007s, a margin that spoke of real single-lap pace. The standout recovery came from Esteban Ocon, who advanced 6 places to finish 9th. Mercedes committed to a one-stop strategy from the outset and Russell drove it to perfection.

2 cars did not make the finish, thinning the points-paying places as the race wore on. Out of the running were Leclerc with an engine that let go and Bearman with damage from a first-lap clash.

Kimi Antonelli holds the points lead on 43, 10 points up on George Russell.

"I knew the gap was there if I could hold the pace, and the team gave me the right call at the right time," said Russell.

### Russell breaks through for a first career victory at the Chinese Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 2*

_George Russell crossed the line first at the Chinese Grand Prix to become a Formula 1 race winner for the first time._

George Russell took the chequered flag at the Chinese Grand Prix to register the first win of his Formula 1 career, a result that felt both earned and overdue in Mercedes' camp by the time the barriers came into view on the final lap.

A maiden win is a threshold crossed only once; Russell crossed it at the Chinese Grand Prix, and the sport recorded it there and then.

"This one means everything, to me and to every single person at Mercedes who put in the work to get here," said Russell.

Other milestones fell at the Chinese Grand Prix as well. Mercedes locked out the top two as well, Russell ahead of Antonelli. A first career pole for Antonelli, who put his name at the top of the timesheet. A first championship points finish for Colapinto, who came home 10th.

### Antonelli holds on for Australian Grand Prix victory

*Race report · Round 1*

_A composed afternoon from Antonelli puts Mercedes on the top step at the Australian Grand Prix._

Kimi Antonelli took victory at the Australian Grand Prix in a drive that answered every question put to him. Max Verstappen crossed the line 22.5s adrift, Lewis Hamilton completing the top three. There was nothing left on the table, 25 points and the win for Antonelli.

Russell took pole but found no answer to Antonelli once the race was underway. Russell had grabbed pole by 0.035s from Antonelli. Esteban Ocon produced the drive of the afternoon, charging from 14th into the points in 8th. The win was built on a one-stop strategy, with Antonelli making the pit calls the rivals could not replicate.

3 cars fell out of contention, the running order shifting with every one of them. It was Colapinto with a gearbox problem, Bortoleto with a power-unit failure and Leclerc with damage from a first-lap clash.

Colapinto sat quietly for a while before facing anyone.

Atop the standings, Kimi Antonelli keeps a 7-point cushion over Max Verstappen.

"The car was there from the start. We just had to execute the plan and not give anything away," said Antonelli.

### A maiden Formula 1 victory for Antonelli at the Australian Grand Prix

*Milestone · Round 1*

_The Australian Grand Prix is where Antonelli became a Grand Prix winner, first across the line and a career changed by it._

The scenes in the Mercedes garage as Antonelli crossed the line told you everything about how long this had been coming; a win that felt inevitable the moment it happened and yet still stopped everyone cold. It was also the first race victory in Mercedes' history as a constructor.

Antonelli's first Formula 1 victory is now part of the record, and the Australian Grand Prix is the answer he will give for the rest of his life when anyone asks where it happened.

Inside the Mercedes garage, the restraint that had held through harder weekends broke the instant the screens confirmed the position was secure.

"We knew the pace was in the car; we just needed one clean race to show it, and the Australian Grand Prix gave us that," said Antonelli.

Alongside the main result, the Australian Grand Prix produced a set of personal markers worth noting. Verstappen and Hamilton joined the ranks of podium finishers for the first time. Russell took pole for the first time, a statement of raw one-lap speed at the right moment. A landmark afternoon for Norris, Piastri, Russell, Hadjar, Ocon, Gasly and Sainz, who all got off the mark in the championship.

### Pre-season pace sets up a 2026 showdown

*Preview · Pre-season*

_The 2026 grid has sorted itself early, with Mercedes at the top and Ferrari the only side close enough to make it a genuine fight._

The gap between Mercedes and the chasing pack is not enormous, but it is real, and in a sport where tenths decide championships, it matters enormously.

The development war will run in parallel with the championship itself, and whichever side keeps its upgrade curve steepest through the flyaway rounds could shift the balance of power before the summer break.

A pre-season pace lead is a head start, not a guarantee, and Ferrari are close enough to make Mercedes pay for any slip.

---

### Front-runners unveiled as 2026 pre-season begins

*Launch · Pre-season*

_From Mercedes to the back of this elite pack, 3 teams believe they have the tools to challenge for wins in 2026._

The cars that will contest race victories in 2026 are no longer a secret. 3 teams with credible championship ambitions have unveiled their contenders, and the engineering statements on show are striking.

Every aero surface on Mercedes' 2026 contender is new, and George Russell and Kimi Antonelli have already logged hours in the simulator working to understand it. The absence of any constructors finish to compare with means the 2026 car sets its own starting line from day one of testing.

Built around a revised suspension geometry that the Ferrari technical staff have been pushing for two seasons, the 2026 car hands Charles Leclerc and Lewis Hamilton a notably different tool. Without a finishing position in the standings to reference, the technical detail on display today is the sole benchmark available.

Lando Norris and Oscar Piastri join McLaren in a year the team has framed internally as a reset, with a new car that carries none of last season's architectural compromises. With no constructors result on the board to measure against, this launch is the only public yardstick on the car.

The launches confirm intent; only laps will confirm whether the engineering has delivered on the winter's promises.

---

### The midfield pack reveals its 2026 weapons

*Launch · Pre-season*

_The midfield is rarely decided at the launch, but 4 teams, Red Bull among them, have given the first clues._

The midfield grid for 2026 is taking shape, with 4 teams now in the open. The margins between them at launch are slim enough that a single aero swing could separate the leaders from the laggards by summer.

Max Verstappen and Isack Hadjar join Red Bull in a year the team has framed internally as a reset, with a new car that carries none of last season's architectural compromises. With no constructors result on the board to measure against, this launch is the only public yardstick on the car.

Pierre Gasly and Franco Colapinto stepped into the simulator for the first time last month and the feedback, by all accounts, was encouraging for everyone at Alpine. No previous constructors campaign provides a frame of reference here; the car and its timing data will have to speak for themselves.

Liam Lawson and Arvid Lindblad are tasked with extracting the maximum from a Racing Bulls package that engineers describe as the most cohesive they have produced in years. The absence of any constructors finish to compare with means the 2026 car sets its own starting line from day one of testing.

Haas pull the covers back on a car that the design team insists solves the rear-stability issues that cost points on the high-speed circuits last year. Without a constructors result to anchor the narrative, the team's ambitions must be read from what the drawing office has actually built.

In the midfield, the launch order is irrelevant; what matters is who has found the most performance when the real season begins.

---

### Ground-up effort on display as backmarker teams launch for 2026

*Launch · Pre-season*

_4 teams at the back of the expected order have launched for 2026, all of them framing the season as a step in a longer journey._

For the 4 teams at the rear of the 2026 order, the launch is the start of a longer process. The cars on show today will look different by mid-season, and that is entirely by design.

Compact, low, and visually striking, Williams' launch car makes a statement before Alexander Albon and Carlos Sainz have turned a wheel in anger. No constructors data exists to set a baseline, which means every lap in testing will be the first hard evidence anyone has.

Audi pull the covers back on a car that the design team insists solves the rear-stability issues that cost points on the high-speed circuits last year. The record books hold no constructors result for this squad, so the 2026 car enters service as an unknown quantity by definition.

Built around a revised suspension geometry that the Aston Martin technical staff have been pushing for two seasons, the 2026 car hands Fernando Alonso and Lance Stroll a notably different tool. With no constructors result on the board to measure against, this launch is the only public yardstick on the car.

Over at Cadillac, Sergio Perez and Valtteri Bottas will carry the hopes of a factory that spent the winter rethinking its aerodynamic philosophy from the floor up. Without a finishing position in the standings to reference, the technical detail on display today is the sole benchmark available.

The gap to the pack ahead has been the winter's primary brief; whether the answers found are sufficient will take a full season to measure.

---

### Lindblad at 19, the rookie Racing Bulls are betting on

*Rookie · Pre-season*

_The step from junior formulae to a full Racing Bulls race seat is the largest of Lindblad's career, and 2026 is where the world finds out whether he's ready for it._

The moment Lindblad steps under the garage lights in parc fermé, he trades the relative shelter of a junior programme for a broadcast audience that dissects every tenth of a second.

Racecraft in traffic is where Formula 1 separates the graduate from the arrival, the braking-reference shift, the understeer in dirty air, the half-second window to commit to a move or abort it, all coming faster than in any category below.

The final verdict on Lindblad's rookie season comes down to whether he closes the gap to his teammate across the year or lets that gap define the conversation heading into contract talks.

---

### Antonelli arrives in F1 at just 19

*Rookie · Pre-season*

_Formula 1 in 2026 hands Kimi Antonelli a seat at Mercedes, a scrutinising global audience, and no margin for a gentle learning curve._

The jump from junior categories to a full Formula 1 season compresses years of technical learning into a winter's worth of preparation, and Antonelli has had to process that acceleration faster than almost any rival on the 2026 grid.

Street circuits arrive without the buffer of long free-practice familiarity, replacing it with a wall on the exit of every barrier-lined chicane and a single shot at the lap.

Sponsor visibility, simulator feedback and raw lap counts will all be weighed as 2026 unfolds to justify or question Mercedes' decision to hand him a front-line seat so early.
